# China Everbright Bank Corporation Limited Notes to the Financial Statements

(Expressed in thousands of Renminbi, unless otherwise stated)

## 1 Company status

China Everbright Bank Corporation Limited (the "Bank") was incorporated in Beijing, the People's Republic of China (the "PRC") on 18 August 1992 as a financial institution and was a wholly owned subsidiary of China Everbright Group Company Limited ("China Everbright Group Company").

Pursuant to the notices "Approval for the restructuring of China Everbright Bank" (YinFu [1995] No. 70) and "Approval for shareholders of China Everbright Bank" (YinGuan [1997] No. 8) issued by the People's Bank of China (the "PBC"), the Bank completed its restructuring and capital expansion on 15 January 1997. The Bank became a joint stock commercial bank and its registered and paid-in capital was increased to RMB 2.8 billion, comprising 2.8 billion ordinary shares of RMB 1 each. Zhong Yin Certified Public Accountants verified the capital and issued a capital verification report on 15 January 1997.

Pursuant to a shareholders' resolution and the approval from the PBC (Yin Fu [1999] No. 278) in 1999, the registered capital of the Bank was increased from RMB 2.8 billion to RMB 4,312 million by transferring RMB 1,512 million from the capital surplus reserve. 1,512 million new ordinary shares of RMB 1 each were issued to the existing shareholders, with 54 new ordinary shares for every 100 existing ordinary shares. The Arthur Anderson – Hua Qiang Certified Public Accountants verified the capital injection and issued a capital verification report on 22 September 2000.

Pursuant to a shareholders' resolution and approval from the PBC (Yin Fu [2000] No.217) in 2000, the Bank issued 1,842 million ordinary shares of RMB 1 each at a price of RMB 1.95 per share on 17 October 2000. The new shares were issued to original shareholders for cash. New shares were issued to new investors if original shareholders waived their rights to subscription. Due to the strong demand for shares, and pursuant to the notice "Approval for the Additional Issuance of Common Shares of China Everbright Bank Corporation Limited" (Yin Fu [2001] No. 147) issued by the PBC, the Bank's common shares issued increased from 1,842 million to 3,157.9 million and the registered capital of the Bank increased from RMB 4,312 million to RMB 7,469.9 million. PricewaterhouseCoopers Zhong Tian Certified Public Accountants verified the increased capital and issued a capital verification report on 2 June 2003.

Pursuant to the shareholders' resolution on 30 April 2002 and approval from the PBC (Yin Fu [2002] No. 119), the registered capital of the Bank was increased from RMB 7,469.9 million to RMB 8,216.89 million by transferring RMB 746.99 million from the capital surplus reserve. 746.99 million new ordinary shares of RMB 1 each were issued to the existing shareholders at the ratio of 1 new share for every 10 existing shares. PricewaterhouseCoopers Zhong Tian Certified Public Accountants verified the capital injection and issued a capital verification report on 3 June 2003.

The principal activities of the Bank are the provision of deposit taking, lending, payment settlement and other financial services in Renminbi ("RMB") and foreign currencies as approved by the China Banking Regulatory Commission (the "CBRC").

The Bank obtained a business license 1000001001174 as approved by the State Administration for Industry and Commerce of the PRC, and a financial service certificate B10711000H0001 as approved by the CBRC.

Besides the Bank's Head Office in Beijing, as at 31 December 2005 the Bank had branches in 23 provinces, autonomous regions and municipalities in the PRC, and a representative office in the Hong Kong Special Administrative Region ("Hong Kong").

## 2 Basis of preparation

These financial statements have been translated into English from the Bank's statutory financial statements issued in the PRC in Chinese.

### (a) The preparation of financial statements

The principal accounting policies adopted in the preparation of the financial statements are in conformity with the Accounting Standards for Business Enterprises and the Accounting Regulations for Financial Enterprises (2001 version) issued by the Ministry of Finance of the PRC ("the MOF").

### (b) Restructuring

In accordance with the Restructuring Plan approved by the State Council of the PRC ("the Restructuring Plan") and announced in August 2007, the Bank carried out the restructuring in 2007, which mainly comprised of debt restructuring and capital injection. The detailed information was set out as follows:

##### (i) Debt Restructuring

According to the debt restructuring agreement entered into on 20 July 2007 among the Bank, China Everbright Group Company and Everbright Finance Ltd, China Everbright Group Company and Everbright Finance Ltd have repaid the debts to the Bank. In addition, part of the Bank's guarantee obligation provided to China Everbright Group Company was relieved and the relief of the remaining guarantee obligation has been agreed in the agreements signed between the debtors and creditors. The details are set out as follows:

(1) As at 31 December 2005, the principal of the Bank's loans due from China Everbright Group Company was RMB 2,118 million equivalent. As at 31 December 2005, these loans were overdue, and the off-balance sheet overdue interest receivable was RMB 579 million. Up to the date of approval for the financial statements, China Everbright Group Company had repaid in full the principal of the loans, while according to the debt restructuring agreement the entire overdue interest as of the date of loan

repayment were waived, and all of China Everbright Group Company's guarantees and pledges provided to the Bank against the principal and interest of the loans were released.

(2) As at 31 December 2005, the principal of the Bank's placement with Everbright Finance Ltd. was RMB 1,367 million equivalent. The placement had been rolled over by means of extension many times, and as at 31 December 2005 the off-balance sheet overdue interest receivable was RMB 355 million. Up to the date of approval for the financial statements, Everbright Finance Ltd. had repaid in full the principal of the placement, while according to the debt restructuring agreement the entire overdue interest as of the date of repayment of placement were waived.

(3) China Everbright Group Company issued financial bonds of RMB 2,100 million in principal with maturity of 3 years in 1997 to Industrial and Commercial Bank of China Limited ("ICBC"), China Construction Bank Corporation Limited ("CCBC"), Agricultural Bank of China ("ABC") and Bank of Beijing. The Bank provided guarantee for the principal and interest of the financial bonds. The bonds were overdue and China Everbright Group Company failed to honour the redemption of the bonds upon maturity. As at 31 December 2005, the Bank's guarantee for the principal and interest in relation to the financial bonds amounted to RMB 3,053 million, including the principal of RMB 2,050 million and interest of RMB 1,003 million. In addition, the loans drawdown by China Everbright Group Company from China Merchants Bank Co., Ltd. ("CMB") and Bank of Beijing were guaranteed by the Bank. As at 31 December 2005, the loan balance was RMB 270 million. Up to the date of approval for the financial statements, China Everbright Group Company has repaid the financial bonds of RMB 250 million due to Bank of Beijing and loans of RMB 270 million due to CMB and Bank of Beijing, and the respective guarantee obligation of the Bank was relieved. For the financial bonds held by ICBC, CCBC and ABC of RMB 600 million each, totally amounting to RMB 1,800 million, and respective interest, related debtors and creditors have signed agreements on the arrangement of relieving the Bank's guarantee obligation.

#### (ii) Capital injection

Under the Restructuring Plan and with the approval of the Bank's Fourth Board of Directors at its fourth provisional meeting held on 13 November 2007 and its shareholders at the first 2007 extraordinary general meeting held on 28 November 2007, the Bank and the Central Huijin Investment Co., Ltd. ("Huijin") reached an Agreement on Investment into the Bank on 28 November 2007. According to the Agreement on Investment into the Bank, Huijin would inject US dollars at an amount of RMB 20 billion equivalent into the Bank to acquire 20 billion new common shares at a price of RMB 1 per share. The capital in US dollars is translated into RMB at the average exchange rate quoted by the PBC at the completion date of the capital injection transaction as defined in the Agreement on Investment into the Bank. In accordance with the revised Articles of Association approved on the meetings of the Bank's Board of Directors and its shareholders as mentioned above, as well as the approval of CBRC, the registered capital of the Bank increased from RMB 8,216.89 million to RMB 28,216.89 million and the common shares issued increased from 8,216.89 million to 28,216.89 million. KPMG Huazhen Certified Public Accountants have verified the increased capital and issued a capital verification report on 3 December 2007.

##### (c) Going Concern

As at 31 December 2005, the Bank accumulated losses of RMB 13,911 million, and its liabilities exceeded its assets by RMB 2,995 million. In view of the implementation of the Restructuring Plan, the Bank's Board of Directors believes that it is appropriate to prepare the financial statements on a going concern basis and it is not necessary to make provision as at 31 December 2005 for the following items:

- the loan principal, as mentioned in the above item (1), with the overdue interest receivable having been excluded from the off-balance sheet items with contingent revenue as set out in Note 53 (b);

- the placement principal, as mentioned in the above item (2), with the overdue interest receivable having been excluded from the off-balance sheet items with contingent revenue as set out in Note 53 (b); and

• the guarantee obligations as mentioned in the above item (3).

#### (d) Changes of accounting policies

On 1 January 2005 the Bank adopted the Accounting Regulations for Financial Enterprises (2001) issued by the MOF in 2001. Prior to that date the Bank adopted the Accounting Regulations for Financial Enterprises (1993) issued by the MOF in 1993. The significant changes in the Bank's accounting policies following the adoption of the Accounting Regulations for Financial Enterprises (2001) are as follows:

(i) In accordance with the "Notice on issue of administration measures for provision accrual and writing-off of bad debts" (Cai Jin [2001] No. 127) issued by the MOF, from 2001 the Bank made provisions for loans by a certain percentage on loan balance over a 5-year period, gradually increasing the provision balance each year. Since 2005, provisions for loan impairment are recognised either on an individual or collective basis by estimating the probable loan losses based on the recoverability of the loan at the year end (please refer to Note 3(g) (iv)).

(ii) In accordance with Cai Jin [2001] No. 127, the Bank made by a certain percentage provisions for repossessed assets over a 5 year period, gradually increasing the provision balance each year. Since 2005 the impairment losses are recognised based on the accounting policy described in Note 3(p).

(iii) From January 2001, overdue interest receivable previously recognised as income before 31 December 2000 was gradually written off against interest income over a 5-year period, and no provisions or write offs through impairment losses was required. Since 2005 the Bank's policy is to discontinue the accrual of interest and record it as an off-balance sheet item when the loan principal or interest is overdue for more than 90 days (including extension). Interest receivable is reversed in the income statement and recorded as an off-balance sheet item. The accounting policy for the interest from placements with banks and other financial institutions is the same as that described above. Please refer to Note 3(t) (i).

(iv) In accordance with Cai Jin [2001] No. 127, since 2001 the Bank has made provisions by a certain percentage for receivables (including deposits and placements with banks and other financial institutions, other receivables and interest receivable) over a 5-year period, gradually increasing the provision balance each year. Since 2005 the impairment losses for those unrecoverable items are recognised on an individual basis (please refer to Note 3(o)).

(v) For short-term investments except government bonds, in accordance with Cai Jin [2001] No. 127, the Bank has made provisions by a certain percentage over a 5-year period, gradually increasing the provision balance each year. Since 2005 short-term investments are carried at the lower of cost and market value. Provisions for diminution in value are recognised for any shortfall in the market value below the cost of short-term investments (please refer to Note 3(j) (i)).

(vi) For long-term investments (except government bonds), in accordance with Cai Jin [2001] No. 127, the Bank has made provisions by a certain percentage over a 5-year period, gradually increasing the provision balance each year. Since 2005 provisions for impairment are recognised for the deficit between the recoverable amount and the carrying amount (please refer to Note 3(p)).

(vii) Previously, the Bank did not make provision for contingent liabilities such as outstanding litigation. Since 2005 provisions are recognised when the Bank has a present obligation as a result of a past event, it is probable that an outflow of economic benefits will be required to settle the obligations and a reliable estimate can be made.

(viii) In accordance with "The Provisions of the MOF on Several Accounting Issues in the Reform of Financial Enterprises Housing System" (Cai Jin [2001] No. 28) issued by the MOF, expenditure for housing reform was amortised over a 5-year period from 2001. Since 2005 such expenditure is recognised in the income statement as incurred.

For the financial statements for the year ended 31 December 2005 the Bank has early adopted the requirements related to goodwill in "Accounting Standards for Business Enterprises No. 20 - Enterprises Combination" issued by the MOF on 15 February 2006. Under the new accounting policy the Bank no longer amortises goodwill, which is stated at cost less provision for impairment. The provision for impairment of goodwill is recognised in accordance with the accounting policy described in Note 3(l) (i).

The changes to accounting policies described above have been applied retrospectively, resulting in an increase in net profit of RMB 1,547 million. The cumulative effect for prior years is set out below:

## 3 Significant accounting policies

(a) Accounting year

The accounting year of the Bank is from 1 January to 31 December.

(b) Reporting currency

The Bank's reporting currency is the RMB.

(c) Accounting basis and measurement principles

The financial statements of the Bank have been prepared on an accrual basis. Unless otherwise stated, the measurement basis used is historical cost.

#### (d) Basis of preparation of the financial statements

The financial statements are consolidated on the basis of the individual financial statements and other financial information of the Head Office, branches and subbranches. All material inter-branch transactions and balances have been eliminated upon combination.

##### (e) Accounting for foreign currency transactions and foreign currency translation

Foreign currency transactions are recorded in multi-currency ledgers. Foreign currency transactions are recorded in their original currency at the transaction date. For the purpose of compiling these financial statements, financial statements denominated in foreign currencies are translated into RMB and then consolidated with the financial statements denominated in RMB.

All balance sheet items denominated in foreign currencies, except for equity, are translated into RMB at the exchange rates ruling at the balance sheet date. Equity items are translated into RMB at the exchange rates ruling at the historical transaction dates. The resulting exchange differences are dealt with as a foreign exchange gain or loss and recorded as capital surplus. The income statement was translated into RMB at the exchange rates ruling at the balance sheet date.

The Bank adopts the exchange rates quoted by the PBC or other exchange rates recognised by the government.

### (f) Cash equivalents

Cash equivalents in the cash flow statement include short-term, highly liquid assets, which are readily convertible into known amounts of cash and are subject to an insignificant risk of changes in value, including the surplus deposit reserve funds with the central bank, and deposits with banks and other financial institutions, placements with banks and other financial institutions and short-term investments with original maturity less than 3 months.

### (g) Loans

#### (i) Loan classification by nature

Loans are classified according to their original maturity terms. Except for loans for which principal or interest is overdue for more than 90 days ("nonaccrual loans"), loans originally maturing within one year are classified as short-term loans, whilst those originally maturing after one year are classified as medium and long-term loans.

Loans are classified as non-accrual loans when the principal or interest is overdue for more than 90 days.

##### (ii) Loan classification by risk

In accordance with the Guidelines on Risk-Based Loan Classification (Yin Fa [2001] No. 416) issued by the PBC, the loans are classified in 5 grades as follows:

Normal: refers to loans where the borrower has been performing its obligations and there are no signs that the borrower will default on principal and interest.

Special mention: refers to loans where the borrower is currently able to make principal and interest payment, but is experiencing factors that may be prejudicial to repayment.

Substandard: Refers to loans where the borrower is clearly experiencing factors impacting its ability to meet payment obligations from its normal business operations. These loans will recognise a loss even after the enforcement of collateral.

- Doubtful: refers to loans where the borrower is unable to meet its payment obligations. These loans will recognise a material loss even after the enforcement of collateral.

Loss: refers to loans where no, or only a minimal amount of principal and interest are collectible after exhausting all collection efforts and all available legal proceedings.

A loan classified as substandard, doubtful or loss is considered non-performing.

##### (iii) Loan recognition

Loans are stated at the amount actually drawn.

##### (iv) Provision for loan impairment

Provision for loan impairment is made if there is objective evidence that the Bank will be unable to collect all amounts due from on and off-balance sheet risk-bearing credits. Credit assets subject to impairment provisioning include loans (including loans secured by tangible assets other than monetary assets, loans pledged by monetary assets, guaranteed loans and unsecured loans), bank card overdrafts, discounted bills, trade finance and credit advances (e.g. bank acceptance bills, guarantees and letters of credit).

Provision for loan impairment are reported as a reduction in the carrying value of a balance sheet item, whereas for off-balance sheet items provisions for credit impairment are reported in other payables. Allowances for credit impairment losses are recognised in the income statements.

Provisions for loan impairment are evaluated either individually or collectively, based on the following principles:

Individual assessment: All significant loans and those with unique credit characteristics are assessed individually for impairment. Loans are deemed to be impaired if management considers that it is probable that the Bank will not be able to collect all amounts of principal and interest due based on the original contractual terms or an equivalent amount. Individual loan exposures are evaluated based upon the borrower's willingness to pay, the payment history, the market value of the security pledged and the ability and intention of the guarantor. The estimated recoverable amount is the present value of expected future cash flows. Impairment is measured at the difference between the carrying amount and the estimated recoverable amount and charged to the income statement.

Collective assessment: Loans which are assessed collectively for impairment include individually assessed loans which are considered significant but with no objective evidence of impairment on an individual basis, and homogeneous groups of loans which are not considered individually significant and not assessed individually. Loans are grouped for similar credit risk characteristics for the purpose of collective assessment. The objective evidence of impairment mainly includes that, though it is unable to identify the decrease of cash flow of individual asset, after collective assessment based on observable data, there is a measurable decrease in the estimated future cash flow, such as adverse changes in the payment status of borrowers in the group or national or local economic conditions that correlate with defaults on the assets in the group.

All impaired loans are reviewed and assessed periodically with any subsequent changes to the estimated recoverable amounts resulting in changes in the allowances for impairment losses.

If, subsequent to the recognition of the impairment losses on loans, there is objective evidence indicating that the value of those assets have recovered which can be related objectively to an event occurring after the impairment was recognised, the previously recognised impairment loss is reversed and recognised in the income statement. The reversal shall not result in a carrying amount of the asset that exceeds the amortised cost at the reversal date had the provision for impairment losses not been made.

##### (v) Loan write-offs

When the loans are deemed to be uncollectible after the completion of legal or other collection procedures, a write-off is made if necessary approval procedures are completed and the loss amount can be reliably estimated. Write-offs are charged against the previously made allowances for impairment losses. Recoveries of amounts previously written off are net against allowances for impairment losses recognised in the current period.

### (h) Discounted bills

Interest income from discounted bills is calculated based on the face value of the bill, the maturity period and the relevant interest rate. As at the balance sheet date, the interest income of discounted bills from the balance sheet date to the maturity date is deferred. Discounted bills are shown on the balance sheet at face value, net of any deferred interest income.

#### (i) Items held under resale and repurchase agreements

In resale and repurchase agreements, the cash advanced or received is recognised as items held under resale and repurchase agreements on the balance sheet. The assets held under resale agreements are recorded as off-balance sheet items, and assets held under repurchase agreements continue to be recognised on the balance sheet.

Depending on the nature of the counterparty, income or expense earned or incurred on these agreements is recognised over the period of the agreement as interest income and expense, or income and expense from deposits and placements with banks and other financial institutions as appropriate.

### (j) Investments

#### (i) Short-term investments

Short-term investments represent investments readily realisable with intended holding periods of less than one year. Short-term investments are carried at the lower of cost and market value. The cost of a short-term investment is the total price paid on acquisition, but does not include unpaid interest on debt securities due at the time of acquisition.

Provisions for diminution in value are made on an item-by-item basis for any shortfall in the market value below cost.

With the exception of interest on debt securities which is due but not yet paid at the time of acquisition, interest is set off against the carrying amount of short-term investments when received by the Bank. Upon disposal or redemption, the difference between the carrying amount of the short-term investment and the proceeds received is recognised in the income statement.

##### (ii) Long-term investments

Long-term investments are investments other than short-term investments.

##### (a) Long-term equity investments

Where the Bank does not control, jointly control or exercise significant influence over an investee enterprise, the investment is accounted for under the cost method whereby it is stated at the initial investment cost. Investment income is recognised when the investee enterprise declares a cash dividend or distributes profits.

Long-term equity investments are accounted for in the balance sheet at cost less provision for impairment losses (Note 3(p)).

##### (b) Long-term debt investments

The initial investment cost of a long-term debt investment is the total price paid on acquisition, excluding any due but unpaid interest receivable at the time of acquisition.

Premiums and discounts are the differences between the par value of the debt investment and the cost, after deducting interest receivable acquired on acquisition. Premiums and discounts are amortised through the income statement over the period from the date of acquisition to the date of maturity using the straight-line method.

Transaction costs associated with the acquisition of long-term debt investments are expensed.

Accrued interest on long-term debt investments with periodic interest payments that are not yet due at the balance sheet date is separately accounted for under interest receivable within current assets. Accrued interest on long-term debt investments with interest payable upon maturity is accounted for under long-term debt investments.

At the period end, long-term debt investments are accounted for in the balance sheet as the principal, plus interest receivable from bonds with interest payable upon maturity, unamortised premiums and discounts, and less any provision for impairment losses (Note 3(p)).

Upon the disposal or transfer of long-term investments, the difference between the proceeds received and the net carrying value after providing for impairment losses is recognised in the income statement.

#### (k) Fixed assets and construction in progress

Fixed assets are assets with comparatively high unit values held by the Bank for developing its banking business. They are expected to be used for more than one year.

Fixed assets are stated in the balance sheet at cost or valuation less accumulated depreciation and provision for impairment losses (Note 3(p)). Construction in progress is stated in the balance sheet at cost less provision for impairment losses (Note 3(p)).

All direct and indirect costs that are related to the construction of fixed assets and incurred before the assets are ready for their intended use are capitalised as construction in progress. Construction in progress is transferred to fixed assets when it is ready for its intended use. No depreciation is provided against construction in progress.

Fixed assets are depreciated using the straight-line method over their estimated useful lives, after taking into account their estimated residual value. The respective estimated useful lives and the estimated rate of residual values on cost for the Bank's fixed assets are as follows:


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Asset category</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Estimated useful life</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Estimated rate of residual value</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Premises</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>30-35 years</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>3%</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Electronic equipment</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>3-5 years</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>3%-5%</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Others</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>5-10 years</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>3%-5%</td></tr></table>

Improvements to leased assets are depreciated using the straight-line method over the shorter of the residual lease period and the estimated useful life of the asset.

Where the Bank leases out assets under operating leases, the assets are included in the balance sheet according to their nature and are depreciated in accordance with the Bank's depreciation policies, as set out above. Impairment losses are provided for in accordance with the accounting policy as set out in Note 3(p). Revenue derived from operating leases is recognised in the income statement using the straight-line method over the lease term.

#### (I) Intangible assets

The Bank's intangible assets include goodwill and other intangible assets.

#### (i) Goodwill

The Bank recognises the difference between the acquisition cost and the fair value of the identifiable assets, liabilities and contingent liabilities acquired as goodwill. Goodwill is stated at cost less any provision for impairment losses and is tested at least annually for impairment. Goodwill is allocated to portfolios of identified assets and the deficit between the recoverable amount of the portfolios and its carrying value is recognised as impairment losses in the income statement. The impairment loss for goodwill that has been recognised in prior periods is not allowed to reverse.

### (ii) Other intangible assets

Other intangible assets are stated at cost less accumulated amortisation and provision for impairment losses (Note 3(p)). The cost or deemed cost of intangible assets is amortised during the beneficial period on a straight-line basis.

### (m) Long-term deferred expenses

Long-term deferred expenses are amortised on a straight-line basis over their beneficial periods. During the beneficial periods, unamortised amounts of any long-term deferred assets that are no longer deemed useful are fully charged to the income statement in the current period.

All expenses incurred during the pre-operating period of branches and subbranches are initially aggregated in long-term deferred expenses and then fully charged to the income statement in the first month the branch or sub-branch commences commercial operations.

### (n) Repossessed assets

Repossessed assets are the physical assets or property rights obtained by the Bank from debtors, warrantors or third parties following the enforcement of its creditor's rights. They are recorded at the amount of the related loan principal, plus any recognised interest receivable and related taxes and expenses arising from the transfer in ownership of the assets. At the same time, all the corresponding loan provisions are transferred to provisions for impairment losses of repossessed assets. At the period end, repossessed assets are stated in the balance sheet at cost less provision for impairment losses (Note 3(p)).

### (o) Provision for bad debts

Provisions for bad debts against all types of receivable items (including deposits and placements with financial institutions, items purchased under resale agreements, interest receivable and other receivables) are made when they are estimated to be unrecoverable. The Bank accounts for bad debt losses through provisioning.

## (p) Provision for impairment

The carrying amounts of assets (including long-term investments, fixed assets, construction in progress, intangible assets except for goodwill, repossessed assets and long-term deferred expenses) are assessed regularly to determine whether their recoverable amounts have declined below their carrying amounts. Assets are tested for impairment whenever events or changes in circumstances indicate that their recorded carrying amounts may not be recoverable.

When such a decline has occurred, provisions for impairment are recognised on an item-by-item basis for the deficit between the recoverable amount and the carrying amount. Changes in the provision for impairment losses are recognised in the income statement when incurred. The recoverable amount is the greater of the net selling price and the present value of the estimated future cash flows arising from the continuous use of the asset and from the disposal of the asset at the end of its useful life.

If there is an indication that there has been a change in the estimates used to determine the provision for impairment losses and as a result the estimated recoverable amount is greater than the carrying amount of the asset, the impairment losses recognised in prior periods are reversed. Reversals of impairment losses are recognised in the income statement. An impairment loss is reversed only to the extent of the asset's carrying amount that would have been determined had no impairment loss been recognised in prior years.

## (q) Subordinated bonds issued

Subordinated bonds issued at par value are recognised as a liability for the total proceeds received upon issuance. The amount of expenses arising from the issuance of the debt securities is recognised in the income statement for the related period.

Interest payable for subordinated bonds issued is recognised on an accrual basis. Interest expenses are recognised in the income statement for the related period.

### (r) Fiduciary activities

The Bank acts in a fiduciary activity as a manager, a custodian or an agent for customers. Assets held by the Bank and the related undertaking to return such assets to customers are excluded from the financial statements as the risks and rewards of the assets reside with customers.

The principal fiduciary activities include entrusted lending and fiduciary wealth management.

The Bank enters into entrusted loan agreements with customers, whereby the customers provide funding ("entrusted funds") to the Bank, and the Bank grants loans to third parties ("entrusted loans") at the instruction of the customers. As the Bank does not assume the risks and rewards of the entrusted loans and the corresponding entrusted funds, the entrusted loans and funds are recorded as off-balance sheet items at their principal amount. No provision for impairment losses is made for entrusted loans.

The Bank develops and sells wealth management products to enterprises and individuals. The associated risks of wealth management products are born by the customers, who receive all income from the products minus a fee for commission.

When the Bank promotes and sells the products, it does not guarantee the customers investment income and does not assume any investment risk or receive income other than commission. The Bank records funds and investments related to these wealth management products as off-balance sheet items.

### (s) Financial derivatives

Financial derivatives arise from foreign currency forwards, foreign currency swaps, interest rate swaps and option transactions undertaken by the Bank. Financial derivatives are recorded at the contract value as off-balance sheet items. The interest of the interest rate swaps is accrued based on the applicable interest rate during the holding period and recognised as interest income or expense, and the profit or loss for other financial derivatives is recognised upon the settlement of the transaction.

## (t) Income recognition

When it is probable that the economic benefits will flow to the Bank and the income can be measured reliably, income is recognised in the income statement according to the following methods:

##### (i) Interest income

Loan interest income is recognised on an accrual basis with reference to the principal and period outstanding and at the rate applicable. For either the loan principal or interest overdue for more than 90 days (including extension), the accrual of interest is discontinued and is accounted for as off-balance sheet items. Interest receivable is reversed in the income statement and recorded as an off-balance sheet item.

Amounts received subsequently will first be set off against the loan principal. It is only when the loan principal is fully recovered that any further amounts received can be recognised in the income statement.

Interest income for placements from banks and other financial institutions is also recognised in the same way as that described for loans.

Other interest income is recognised on an accrual basis with reference to the principal and period outstanding and at the applicable rate.

##### (ii) Fee and commission income

Fee and commission income is recognised when the related services are rendered.

#### (iii) Other income

Other income is recognised on an accrual basis.

## (u) Expense recognition

#### (i) Interest expense

Interest expenses are recognised on an accrual basis with reference to the amount and period outstanding and at the rate applicable.

#### (ii) Other expenses

Other expenses are recognised on an accrual basis.

### (v) Employee benefits

The Bank participates in social insurance programs established by government agencies, including pension plans, medical insurance, housing provident funds and other social insurance. According to the relevant regulations, insurance premiums and housing provident funds are provided on a certain percentage of the prescribed basis and contributed to labour and social insurance organisations. Such expenses are recognised in the income statement as incurred.

In addition, in accordance with the "Notice on filing of Enterprises Annuity Plan" (Lao She Ting Han [2006] No. 22) issued by the Ministry of Labour and Social Security, the Bank has operated an employee annuity plan since 1 January 2005. The annuity funds are contributed by the Bank and qualified employees. The Bank contributes by 6%-8% of the employees' total salary annually. Contributions are recognised as an operating expense in the income statement as incurred.

Furthermore, the Bank has an obligation in respect of supplementary retirement benefits for its employees who retired on or before 31 December 2015. The expense is recognised in the income statement as incurred.

Except the above, there are no significant responsibilities for the payments of employment retirement benefit and other post retirement benefit.

### (w) Operating leases

Rental payments under operating lease are charged to the income statement as expenses on a straight-line basis over the period of the lease.

### (x) Income tax

The Bank uses the tax-effect accounting method to account for income tax. Income tax for the year comprises current tax paid and payable and movements in deferred tax assets and liabilities.

Current income tax is calculated at the applicable tax rate on taxable income.

Deferred tax is provided for using the liability method for the differences between the accounting profits and the taxable profits arising from timing differences in recognising income, expenses or losses. Deferred tax is only recognised to the extent that it is probable that the asset can be utilised in the future. When the tax rate changes or a new type of tax is levied, adjustments are made to the amounts originally recognised for the timing differences under the liability method. The current tax rates are used in arriving at the reversal amounts when the timing differences are reversed.

Deferred tax assets can only be recognised to the extent that the related tax benefits are expected to be realised.

### (y) Provisions and contingent liability

Provisions are recognised when the Bank has a present obligation as a result of a past event, it is probable that an outflow of economic benefits will be required to settle the obligations and a reliable estimate can be made.

Where it is not probable that the settlement of the above obligation will cause an outflow of economic benefits, or the amount of the outflow cannot be estimated reliably, the obligation is disclosed as a contingent liability.

### (z) Dividends

Dividends are recognised as a liability in the year in which they are approved and declared.

#### (aa) Related parties

If the Bank has the power, directly or indirectly, to control, jointly control or exercise significant influence over another party, or vice versa, or where the Bank and one or more parties are subject to common control from another party, they are considered to be related parties. Related parties may be individuals or enterprises.

## 4 Taxes

The Bank's main applicable taxes and tax rates are as follows:

(a) Business tax

Business tax is charged at 5% on taxable income.

(b) City construction tax

City construction tax is calculated as 1% - 7% of business tax.

(c) Education surcharge

Education surcharge is calculated as 3% of business tax.

## (d) Income tax

The Bank's income tax is paid centrally by the Head Office and the applicable rate is 33%.

##### (a) Receivables for World Bank's on-lending and collection fee

Included in the assets and liabilities acquired from the former China Investment Bank ("the former CIB") on 18 March 1999 were USD 853 million (equivalent to RMB 7,056 million) of World Bank on-lending loans and related interest receivable balances ("World Bank on-lending"), and USD 586 million (equivalent to RMB 4,852 million) of borrowings. The difference of USD 267 million (equivalent to RMB 2,204 million) between World Bank on-lending and related borrowings was because the former CIB used its own funds to facilitate those on-lending loans.

On 18 August 2000 the MOF issued the notice (Cai Jin [2000] No. 61) "Response Regarding the Granting of Supportive Financial Policies to China Everbright Group Limited", allowing the Bank to treat the outstanding on-lending loans of USD 853 million as off-balance sheet items. In addition, the MOF required the Bank to set up specific accounts for the recovery of World Bank on-lending loans and to turn in any proceeds recovered to the MOF.

In accordance with the above notice, the Bank derecognised the outstanding World Bank on-lending loans of RMB 7,056 million and related borrowings of RMB 4,852 million, and recorded the difference of RMB 2,204 million in other receivables.

On 9 May 2003 the MOF issued the notice (Cai Ji Han [2003] No. 13) "Consideration on Receivables of China Everbright Bank for World Bank's on-lending by the MOF". In accordance with the notice, amounts received from World Bank on-lending was to be shared, between the Bank and the MOF by receiving 32% and 68% respectively, to recover the amount recorded by the Bank in other receivables. Reasonable expenses incurred for the collection of World Bank on-lending were to be disbursed to the Bank by the MOF after examination.

The Bank makes provision for the amounts due from World Bank on-lending loans based on their estimated recoverable amounts and the above allocation ratio. Management believes that the provision recognised is reasonable and adequate.

##### (b) Receivables from fiduciary wealth management business

The fiduciary wealth management business of the Bank mainly represents the service to sell self-developed wealth management products to enterprises and individuals. The credit risk, liquidity risk and interest risk of the underlying debt securities investments are assumed by the enterprises and individuals who purchase the products. The Bank earns commission fee through the transactions. Fiduciary assets and liabilities are not the Bank's assets and liabilities and hence, not recognised in the balance sheet of the Bank. As the Bank must purchase bond investments first and then sell the wealth management products to customers, the maturities of the Bank's underlying debt investments may not match with those of the wealth management products completely and there is a timing difference. This results in a difference between the balances of wealth management assets and liabilities. The advances to customers are recorded in other receivables and the payables to customers are recorded in other payables (Note 35).

##### (a) Goodwill arising from takeover of the former CIB

As approved by the PBC, the Bank and CDB jointly signed the "Agreement between China Development Bank and China Everbright Bank for the Transfer of Assets, Liabilities and Banking offices of the Former China Investment Bank" (the "Agreement") on 18 March 1999. According to the Agreement, CDB transferred the assets, liabilities and equity of 29 branches and 137 outlets of the former CIB to the Bank. The Agreement became effective on 18 March 1999. Pursuant to the Agreement, the Bank recorded the transferred assets and liabilities at the carrying amount as recorded by the former CIB at the effective date of the Agreement. In 2001, in accordance with "the MOF's Consideration on Recognition of Goodwill in China Everbright Bank's acquisition of the former China Investment Bank" (Cai Jin Han [2001] No. 20) issued by the MOF, the Bank reassessed the transferred assets and liabilities at fair value, and recognised the excess of the purchase cost over the sum of the fair value of the net assets transferred and deferred tax asset as goodwill of RMB 6,537 million.

In 2004 and before goodwill was amortised on a straight-line basis through the income statement over 20 years. As described in Note 2, the Bank ceased amortisation of the goodwill in its financial statements since 2005. The accumulated amortisation of RMB 518 million at 1 January 2005 was eliminated against the cost of goodwill at that date.

The Bank allocates goodwill to portfolios of identified assets. The recoverable amount of portfolios of identified assets is determined on the basis of value-in-use calculation, which is based on the following assumptions:

Discount rate used for value-in-use calculation 12%

Long-term growth rate 8.5%

Based on the above impairment review, the Bank believes that the provision recognised is reasonable and adequate and no further provision is required.

<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Recognised As at 1 January 2005</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Recognised As at 31 December 2005</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Impairment for the former CIB assets (i)</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>3,153,250</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2,436,035</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Impairment for other loans and assets (ii)</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2,029,063</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>1,936,409</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>Overdue interest receivable (iii)</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>315,786</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>-</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>TOTAL</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>5,498,099</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>4,372,444</td></tr></table>

(i) Pursuant to the "Notice on Tax Issues regarding Write-Off and Disposal of Non-performing Assets of China Everbright Bank" (Cai Shui [2002] No. 198) issued by the MOF and the State Administration of Taxation in 2002, the Bank adopted the following tax treatments when writing off and disposing of non-performing assets which were taken over from the former CIB:

During the period from 2001 to 2009, the Bank may write off doubtful loans and dispose of non-performing assets of the former CIB by setting them off against the specific provision of RMB 14,400 million made for the non-performing assets transferred from the former CIB.

During the period mentioned above, actual losses incurred from the non-performing assets of the former CIB can be deducted from the Bank's taxable income upon obtaining approval from the relevant tax authorities.

Therefore, the balance of the provision for doubtful loans and non-performing assets is deductible for taxation purposes when the Bank writes off the doubtful loans and non-performing assets in subsequent years. The tax effect arising from the significant timing difference is recognised as a deferred tax asset.

(ii) In addition to the impairment losses on assets acquired from the former CIB as described in note (i), the Bank also made allowances for impairment losses on other customer loans and assets in accordance with its accounting policies. The allowances for impairment losses are determined based on the expected recoverable amount of the relevant assets at the balance sheet date. However, the amounts deductible for tax purposes are calculated at 1% of the gross carrying amount of qualifying assets at the balance sheet date, together with write-offs which fulfil specific criteria as set out in the PRC tax rules, and are approved by the tax authorities.

(iii) As described in Note 2, interest receivable accrued before 31 December 2000 was reversed in the income statement when it, or the corresponding loan principal, became overdue for more than 90 days, in accordance with the Bank's accounting policy. Such adjustments of interest income were deductible for tax purposes over 5 years, beginning from 2001.

## 39 Subordinated bonds issued

With the approval of the CBRC, the Bank issued subordinated bonds on 23 June 2004 at par value of RMB 5,550 million with a term of 6 years and to be matured on 23 June 2010. The subordinated bonds bear interest at a floating rate based on the PBC's one year fixed deposit rate plus a margin of 2.61%. The one-year fixed deposit rate is reset by reference to the fixed deposit rate quoted by the PBC in the fifth day before the date of interest payment and the margin remained unchanged till maturity. The Bank should not redeem the subordinated bonds in cash or in kind before maturity unless obtaining the approval of the CBRC.

##### (a) Waived interest

Waived interest mainly includes waived interest payable on takings from CDB:

According to the notice (Cai Jin [2002] No. 186) issued by the MOF in 2003, "The Approval of the Issues Related to RMB 5,000 million Placement to China Everbright Bank from CDB", the MOF approved the waiving of interest owed by the Bank to CDB on the takings of RMB 5,000 million from CDB. The Bank transferred the interest payable of RMB 418 million to capital surplus in 2003.

In accordance with the reply from Beijing Xi Cheng District State Taxation Bureau on 30 July 2003 "Tax Issues Relating to the Relief of China Everbright Bank's Interest Payable to CDB", the Bank's waived interest payable to CDB was exempted from income tax payment. Therefore, the Bank does not need to accrue for income tax arising from the aforementioned capital surplus recognised.

## 50 Capital adequacy ratio

The Bank's capital and core capital adequacy ratios were calculated in accordance with the "Notice on Issuing Statistics Guidance on Capital Adequacy Ratio" (Yin Jian Tong [2004] No. 18) as promulgated by the CBRC, and were set out as follows:

Related parties without controlling relationships comprised of the affiliated entities of China Everbright Group Company and the shareholders, excluding China Everbright Group Company, who appointed directors to the Bank. (i) As at 31 December 2005, the principal of loans granted to China Everbright Group Company was RMB 2,118 million equivalent. As stated in Note 2, up to the date of approval for the financial statements the Bank has collected the principal of the loans in full. Therefore no provision was accrued for the loans' principal.

(ii) As at 31 December 2005, the principal of placement with Everbright Finance Ltd. was RMB 1,367 million equivalent. As stated in Note 2, up to the date of approval for the financial statements the Bank has collected the principal of the placement in full. Therefore no provision was accrued for the placement's principal.

(iii) As at 31 December 2005, the major guarantee provided to China Everbright Group Company was the guarantee for financial bonds with principal and interest of RMB 3,053 million with maturity of 3 years issued by China Everbright Group Company in 1997 and the guarantee for loans of RMB 270 million. As stated in Note 2, up to the date of approval for the financial statements, the Bank's guarantee obligations for the outstanding principal of the financial bonds of RMB 250 million and loans of RMB 270 million have been relieved, and the relief arrangement of the Bank's guarantee obligation for the remaining financial bonds of RMB 1,800 million and respective interest has been agreed in the agreements signed between the debtors and creditors. Therefore, no provision was accrued for the guarantee obligations.

(iv) Others include other shareholders which appointed directors to the Bank up to the balance sheet date.

Management of the Bank believes that the transactions with related parties are entered in ordinary course of business at the relevant market rates and follow the standard business procedures.

## 52 Entrusted businesses

### (a) Entrusted lending business

The Bank provides entrusted lending business services to government agencies, corporations and individuals. All entrusted loans are funded by entrusted funds from them. The Bank generally does not take on any credit risk in relation to these transactions. The Bank acts as an agent to hold and manage these assets and liabilities at the direction of the entrustor and receives fee income for the services provided. The entrusted assets are not the assets of the Bank and are not recognised in the balance sheet. Surplus funds are accounted for as deposits from customers.


<table border=1 style='margin: auto; word-wrap: break-word;'><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'></td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2005</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>2004</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>ENTRUSTED LOANS</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>16,921,068</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>15,209,790</td></tr><tr><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>ENTRUSTED FUNDS</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>16,921,068</td><td style='text-align: center; word-wrap: break-word;'>15,209,790</td></tr></table>

### (b) Fiduciary wealth management business

The Bank sells self-developed wealth management products to enterprises and individuals. The credit risk, liquidation risk and interest risk of the underlying debt securities investments are assumed by the enterprises and individuals who purchase the products. The Bank earns commission fees through the transactions. The corresponding fiduciary assets and liabilities are not the Bank's assets and liabilities and are not recognised in the balance sheet. The difference between the holding period of the underlying investments and the period of the wealth management products results in the mismatch in the wealth management assets and liabilities. The surplus assets are treated as receivables from customers and recorded in other receivables (Note 12), the surplus liabilities are treated as payables to customers and recorded in other payables (Note 35).

##### Note:

On 11 October 2005, with the approval of the PBC, the Bank and CDB entered into a 10-year interest swap agreement with a notional amount of RMB 5,000 million. The purpose of the interest swap transaction is to offset the RMB interest risk of the Bank's fixed rate long-term assets. Under the agreement, the Bank pays interest at a fixed rate and CDB pays interest at a floating rate. As at 31 December 2005 the revaluation of the swap's fair value is a loss to the Bank of RMB 16.6 million. The change of the fair value of hedging assets related to the swap transaction is a gain of RMB 65.45 million.

### (ii) Irrevocable lending businesses recorded off-balance sheet

Acceptances comprise undertakings by the Bank to pay bills of exchange drawn on customers. The Bank's management expects most acceptances to be settled simultaneously with the reimbursement from customers.

The Bank provides financing guarantees and letters of credit to guarantee the performance of customers to third parties.

The amount reflected in the table below for guarantees and letters of credit represents the maximum loss that would be recognised at the balance sheet date if counterparties failed completely to perform as contracted.

## 54 Contingent liabilities

### (a) Outstanding litigations and disputes

As at 31 December 2005, the Bank was the defendant in certain pending litigation and disputes. Provisions (Note 35) have been made for the estimated losses of such litigation based upon the opinions of the Bank's internal and external legal counsellors. The Bank's management considers that the provisions made are reasonable and adequate.

Contracted for

### (d) Underwriting and redemption obligations

The Bank's unexpired commitments for underwriting bonds as at 31 December 2005 amounted to RMB 500 million.

The Bank has the responsibility to pay the principal and interest of government bonds sold by it upon redemption. The redemption price for a bond at any time before its maturity date is based on the coupon value plus any interest unpaid and accrued up to the redemption date. The redemption price for bonds redeemed early may not be the same as the market value of the similar financial instruments trading at the same date. The Bank collects the principal and interest from the MOF at the maturity date for any bonds redeemed early.

The redemption obligations (nominal values) of the Bank as at each balance sheet date are as follows:

## 56 Post balance sheet events

#### (a) Restructuring arrangement

The Bank carried out restructuring in 2007 in accordance with the Restructuring Plan. For detailed information, please refer to Note 2.

### (b) Changes in accounting standards

According to the resolution of the Fifth Session of the Forth Board of Directors, the Bank will implement the Accounting Standards for Business Enterprises issued by the MOF on 15 February 2006 ("the new ASBE"), effective on 1 January 2007, which is substitute for the existing Accounting Standards for Business Enterprises and Accounting Regulations for Financial Enterprises ("the existing ASBE"). The Bank will change the existing accounting policies and estimations prepared under the existing ASBE according to the new ASBE. The implementation of the new ASBE may have impact on the financial position and the result of operations of the Bank.

#### (c) Changes in income tax rate

On 16 March 2007, the Fifth Plenary Session of the Tenth National People's Congress passed the Corporate Income Tax Law of the People's Republic of China ("the new tax law") which will take effect on 1 January 2008. As a result of the new tax law, it is expected that the income tax rate applicable to the Bank will be reduced from 33% to 25% from 1 January 2008. The enactment of the new tax law is not expected to have any financial effect on the amounts accrued in the balance sheet in respect of current tax payable as at 31 December 2005. The Bank is in the process of collecting information to assess the impact on the financial position as a result of the above changes in the enterprise income tax law for the year 2007 and thereafter.

## 57 Comparative information

The financial statements of the Bank for the year ended 31 December 2004 are unaudited.
